Cherishvale · Unlisted family research · Attorney & title-examiner briefing

Corbello–Granger
Land & Mineral Records

An objective, source-indexed title and heirship briefing concerning Division / Block No. 3 (300 acres) of the Estate of Treville Granger Sr. in the William Dyson League, Abstract No. 10, Orange County, Texas, prepared for legal and title examination.

Updated October 19, 2026 · Complete Equity 234 NARA file & 1888 Partition Plat located in OPR #291245; all 8 Corbello branches documented

300 acresDivision 3 · Probate No. 15 (Book E pp. 147–150, Feb. 10, 1888; Plat in OPR #291245)
100+ PDFs67 local family PDFs + 34 county-portal deed, probate & court pulls
Equity 234Full 25-page NARA packet & Plat located at OPR #291245 (Dec. 13, 2005)

Evidentiary standard for counsel review: This briefing strictly separates (1) verified court decrees, probate minutes, and contemporaneous recorded conveyances from (2) later recorded affidavits of heirship, adverse-possession notices, and third-party production listings. Recording or microfilm certification does not establish every assertion in a later affidavit as true. Where post-1985 family affidavits conflict with 1883–1931 court and deed records, both are cited with exact Volume, Page, and Instrument numbers so counsel can verify each link directly.

Sources, attribution, and limits of this document. Every factual statement here is drawn from a public court record, a public agency record (Railroad Commission of Texas, county appraisal districts, state unclaimed-property registries), or a published opinion, and each is cited to its volume, page, instrument number, or docket so that it can be independently verified and, where needed, certified. Where two records conflict, this briefing reports the conflict and does not resolve it — the resolution is a matter for a title examiner and the court. Statements about the content of a recorded instrument are statements about that document, not characterizations of the person who signed it. Named individuals appear in the capacities in which the cited public records place them: as affiants, notaries, grantees, or beneficiaries. This is a research compilation prepared to assist counsel, not a title opinion, not a determination of legal entitlement, and not a finding of fraud or misconduct by any person. No conclusion of wrongdoing by any individual is asserted on this page, and none should be read into it. Statements about the 2001 instruments, the 1985 questioned-document letter and the 1924 judgment are records and open questions, not conclusions: whether any person acted unlawfully is for a court to determine, on advice of counsel. If any statement here is believed inaccurate, the underlying record governs and the page should be corrected to match it.

Unlisted means accessible by link, not password-protected. Search engines are instructed not to index this page.

Executive summary of October 19, 2026 breakthroughs: Primary records retrieved from the Orange County Clerk’s Imaged Books and Official Public Records now establish: (1) Treville Granger Sr. died November 30, 1881, and his estate was partitioned on February 10, 1888 in Orange County Probate Case No. 15, Probate Minutes Book E, pages 147–150, allotting the middle 300-acre strip (Division No. 3) to Erisse Carbello (plat recorded in OPR #291245, p. 18); (2) on September 8, 1888 (recorded Oct. 7, 1901), Erisse and Charles Carbello signed a deed of Block 3 to her brother Tresimond Granger for $450; (3) in 1921–1925, acting through attorney-in-fact and next friend John L. Wasey (who received an undivided 1/2 interest for his services and outlay under an April 1, 1916 power of attorney, DR Vol. 22 p. 507), Erisse obtained a federal default decree against nine defendants in Equity No. 234 (DR Vol. 35 pp. 75–77; Vol. 41 pp. 210–212; full NARA file in OPR #291245) and a comprehensive state-court settlement judgment on October 1, 1924 in 60th Dist. Ct. No. 23895 (DR Vol. 40 pp. 204–208) finding she had been of unsound mind since September 1, 1888, ratifying Gulf Production Co.’s leases on the 50-acre and 29-acre tracts in return for 1/5 of the 1/6 royalty (a 1/30 royalty) and 1/5 of bonuses/minerals, and awarding the balance of Block 3 half to Erisse and half to Wasey; and (4) after Erisse’s interdiction (1925) and death (February 3, 1930), seven of her eight children joined in November 1931 mineral deeds to J. H. Harlan (DR Vol. 52 pp. 123, 130, 171), reserving a 1/96 net royalty on the 50-acre Subdivision 3 tract.

1A. Where the research came from

This map traces the places and repositories identified in this briefing. Select a box for its source notes and remaining gaps. “Retrieved” describes the acquisition of a record, not proof that every statement in it is true. This inventory summarizes the October 18–19 research log; no new courthouse search was performed for this map.

Orange County, Texas

Direct county pulls

Sources: County Clerk Imaged Books, Official Public Records, and probate index. Core citations: DR 35:75–77, DR 40:204–208, DR 43:497–500, DR 52:123/130/171, OPR #291245, #227435 / #227585, and #298552–#298565. OCAD appraisal results were reported by the other research window; mineral ownership is unresolved.

Gap: Probate index references are not the underlying minute pages. Obtain Book E pp. 131, 143–150 and guardianship accounts in Books J/K/L; complete the later title and payment chain.

County records portal · Record findings & citations · Outstanding files

Jefferson County / Beaumont, Texas

Copies via Orange County

Origin: 60th Judicial District Court, Jefferson County, Cause 23895 (Oct. 1, 1924); federal Eastern District of Texas, Beaumont Division, Equity 234 and 241.

Access: Judgment and federal-file reproductions retrieved from Orange County deed/OPR records. This does not mean the Jefferson District Clerk or federal court independently supplied a fresh file. Request the state pleadings, exhibits and docket to compare with the recorded judgment.

1924 judgment and federal packet · Federal case scope

Calcasieu Parish, Louisiana

Mixed reviewed / outstanding

Reviewed: The published Corbello v. Corbello opinion and supplied case PDF describe Erisse’s interdiction, bank curatorship and accounts. A separate collateral-family tutorship copy appears in Orange County DR 42:74–78. Lake Charles family events are also recited in affidavits, obituary and genealogy material.

Gap: The original parish interdiction, succession and curator-account files have not been obtained. USGenWeb succession snippets remain unverified leads.

Opinion and financial figures · Parish records to obtain

Fort Worth / Tarrant County, Texas

Federal archive request sent

Repository: National Archives at Fort Worth, RG 21, Beaumont equity files, dockets and minutes. Request sent Oct. 19, 2026; automated acknowledgment recorded.

Already in hand: A 2005 NARA-certified reproduction of Equity 234, retrieved from Orange County OPR #291245. The briefing documents no substantive NARA reply to the 2026 request. Tarrant County is the repository location, not the land location or an additional county-deed search.

Request text and status · NARA collection identifiers

Liberty County, Texas

Archive request sent

Repository: Sam Houston Regional Library and Research Center, Liberty. Request sent Oct. 19, 2026 for historical Orange County records.

Gap: No returned records are documented here. Liberty is an archive location; no Liberty County land-title connection has been established. The original request’s “no administration” premise was superseded by the Orange County probate-index findings.

Submitted archive request · Later probate findings

St. Landry Parish / Opelousas, Louisiana

Lead only · not collected

Purpose: Early Granger / LeBleu conveyances, donations, probate and possible marriage records. The briefing lists the parish archive as a follow-up source.

Status: No parish record pull is documented. A birthplace or family association does not establish that a relevant instrument is held there.

Proposed parish searches

Reproduced: GLO Spanish Archives Vol. 22 pp. 303–305 and certification appear in the family/recorded trustee-deed material; a fresh, legible GLO copy is still needed. ClaimItTexas is a proposed search route, not a documented recovered balance. Austin is an agency location, not a separate county-record pull.

Official RRC results · GLO and examiner material · Published opinions

Louisiana statewide sources

Published judicial opinion read

Reviewed: Louisiana Supreme Court, Corbello v. Corbello, 171 La. 735, 132 So. 127, through a published reproduction and supplied PDF.

Still needed: Certified vital records and original parish accounts. Louisiana Vital Records and LaCashClaim are follow-up routes; this briefing does not document retrieved certificates or located unclaimed funds. A state office’s mailing address is not another parish search.

Published opinion · Vital-record follow-up

Other places, supplied files & web leads

Context / secondary sources

Harris County / Houston, TX: Lee Hager’s residence is recited in the federal complaint; no Harris County deed pull is documented. Asheboro, NC: a third-party MineralHolders owner listing was reviewed; no North Carolina courthouse pull or verified genealogical link follows from it.

Lafayette, LA, and New Llano, LA: places mentioned in family filings, not documented local record searches.

Acquisition and review log · Genealogy source limitations · Web leads and exclusions

The disputed-claim question: start with the document comparisons

The strongest report identifies a particular statement, the exact instrument containing it, the conflicting record, and any evidence of reliance or loss. A discrepancy can justify investigation without establishing knowing falsity or present-day fraud. The places above identify record sources; they do not establish where a modern offense occurred or which office has jurisdiction.

Issue to examineRecords to compareWhat remains to establish
2001 Robert Larry affidavits: sole-heir claim Document conflict confirmed Oct. 21OPR 1248:888 #227435 and 1249:491 #227585 against the 1930 opinion/obituary, DR 52:123/130/171 and earlier branch affidavits. Full comparison in Section 13C.Quote the exact statement from each instrument; identify the signer, claimed basis of knowledge, recipient and any transaction or payment relying on it. Obtain certified copies and the underlying vital records.
Historical signatures / alleged forgery1985 examiner’s letter reproduced in the 1986 trustee deed, OPR 624:211–215 #39960, alongside the original Spanish Archives instrument and comparison specimens. Examiner material.The letter is an examiner’s opinion, not a forgery judgment. Obtain legible originals, full report, specimen provenance and any subsequent judicial findings.
Trustee authority, accounting and distributionsOPR 624:211–215 #39960; later trustee conveyances, trust agreement, beneficiary lists, accounts and payment records. Trustee follow-up.Authority, duties, actual receipts/disbursements, beneficiaries and specific unexplained transactions. Missing material in this research file does not establish that no accounting exists.
Historical adjudicated conduct versus a modern allegationOct. 1, 1924 judgment, DR 40:204–208, and Equity 234 complaint/decrees in OPR #291245. Judgment context.Separate allegations in a complaint from findings and relief in the judgment. A historical finding concerning a deed does not itself prove wrongdoing by a current owner or operator.
Possible unpaid / diverted royalties1924 compromise, 1931 reservations, later title chain, lease/unit records, division orders, suspense ledgers and actual payor statements; RRC 03-03243 is a regulatory lead. Wellbore evidence.Current entitlement, tract-to-well match, payor, payment period, amounts due/paid and any actual loss. Production estimates or an owner listing alone do not show diverted funds.

Suggested report packet: a one-page factual chronology; an exhibit index with county/parish, book/page or instrument number and retrieval source; complete copies with disputed passages marked; the conflicting records; and any correspondence, division orders or payment evidence. Ask the receiving investigator or counsel to assess the suspected conduct and appropriate jurisdiction. Preserve the complete originals separately from annotated working copies.

Coverage assembled from sections 4–16 of this briefing on October 21, 2026. Earlier sections contain dated interim statuses; the later October 19 retrieval notes control where they document a subsequently located record. This map does not independently revalidate the earlier legal conclusions or claimed ownership fractions.

2. Master chronological timeline of title, litigation & heirship (1835–2026)

This chronological digest synthesizes all verified deeds, probate entries, federal and state court decrees, and later heirship filings examined to date. Primary recorded instruments and court judgments are tagged Verified Record; later family affidavits and self-serving notices are tagged Claimant Filing.

DateEvent / Instrument / ProceedingRecording / Docket CitationLegal & Title Significance
Feb. 13, 1835Coahuila & Texas headright league grant to William Dyson on Cow Bayou, Orange County, Texas (Abstract No. 10).Spanish Archives Vol. 22, pp. 303–305 (GLO cert. June 17, 1986 in OPR Vol. 624 p. 212)Verified Record Sovereign patent / headright source of title for the William Dyson League (A-10).
Sept. 10, 1857William & Delaide Ashworth convey 900 acres in the William Dyson League (east side of Cow Bayou, reserving 211 acres in the NE corner) to Moise L. LeBleu, who conveys in lifetime to his brother-in-law Treville Granger Sr.Recited in Deed Record Book H, pp. 560–561 & DR Vol. 34, p. 460Verified Record Establishes the 900-acre LeBleu/Granger tract (part of Treville Granger Sr.’s ~1,500 acres in the Dyson League) and separates the 211-acre NE Ashworth Reserve where the collateral Esperance Corbello / Michel branch later held 8.5-acre tracts.
May 1879Death of eldest son Treville Granger Jr. (husband of Cecile Chesson Granger), leaving five minor children: Martin Jr., T. R. (Tresimond), Modesta (Peveto), Mary (Norwood), and Madline (Wilkerson).DR Vol. 35, p. 137 (1922); OPR Vol. 745, p. 728 (1937 Frank Granger statement)Verified Record Explains why Jr.’s five children took one 300-acre share (Division No. 5) collectively through their mother and guardian, Cecile Granger.
Nov. 30, 1881Death of Jean Treville Granger Sr. intestate, survived by five branches: (1) heirs of Treville Jr., dec’d; (2) Michael “Bishon” Granger; (3) Tresimond Granger; (4) Marguerite Erisse Granger Corbello (wife of Manuel Carlos “Charles” Corbello); and (5) Martin Granger. (A sixth child, Frank, died ca. 1865 age 17, unmarried.)OPR Vol. 745, p. 728 (1937 statement); Probate Case No. 15 (filed Mar. 19, 1883)Verified Record Fixes the true date of death (superseding the “about 1887” approximation in Chesson v. La Flore and the 1921 Bill of Complaint) and establishes the five 1/5 inheritance branches (300 acres each out of 1,500 acres).
Mar. 19, 1883Administration opened on the Estate of Treville Granger, Deceased in Orange County Probate Court; son Tresimo [Tresimond] Granger appointed Administrator (Bond & Oath May 21, 1883).Orange County Probate Case No. 15; Probate Minutes Book D, pp. 170, 181, 362 (Index Sheet 70)Verified Record Proves formal probate administration occurred in Orange County (correcting Frank Granger’s 1937 recollection that there was no administration).
June 2, 1883
(rec. June 25, 1883)		Confirmation Deed: Moise L. LeBleu (Calcasieu Parish, LA) to the Heirs of Treville Granger Sr., dec’d (Heirs of Treville Granger Jr. dec’d, Michael Granger, Trasimond Granger, Erisse Corbello [Granger], and Martin Granger) for 900 acres, Wm. Dyson League, confirming the deed made to Treville Sr. in his lifetime.Deed Record Book H, pp. 560–561Verified Record Contemporaneous recorded deed vesting record title in the five named branches, explicitly naming Erisse Corbello (Granger) as one of the five co-heirs.
Aug. 3, 1885Cecile Granger appointed guardian of the minor heirs of Treville Granger Jr. (Modesty, Izami/Mary, Tresimo & Martene).Probate Minutes Book D, pp. 252–254, 311; Book E, pp. 150, 366 (Index Sheet 70)Verified Record Establishes the guardianship represented on Division No. 5 of the 1888 Partition Plat.
Jan. 6, 1888Lunacy Proceedings: Estate of Eddie [Erisse] Granger, N.C.M. (Non Compos Mentis) entered in Orange County Probate Court one month before the estate partition.Probate Minutes Book E, p. 131 (Index Sheet 70)Verified Record Contemporaneous 1888 probate record corroborating the 1924 court finding that Erisse was of unsound mind by 1888.
Feb. 10, 1888DECREE & REPORT OF PARTITION OF THE ESTATE OF TREVILLE GRANGER SR.: Amended final account (E 143), Citation (E 145), Final account approved (E 146), Order for Partition (Book E, p. 147), Report of Partition (Book E, p. 147), and Partition Approved (Book E, p. 150), surveyed by County Surveyor William Morgan, dividing the 1,500 acres into five 300-acre divisions and setting aside Division / Block / Share No. 3 (300 acres) to Erisse Granger Carbello.Probate Case No. 15; Probate Minutes Book E, pp. 143–150 (Index Sheet 70; Plat in OPR #291245, p. 18; cited in DR 15:551, 40:206, 43:499)Verified Record The foundational title instrument for this claim: vests separate fee-simple title to the 300-acre Division / Block No. 3 in Erisse Granger Corbello.
Sept. 8, 1888
(not recorded until Oct. 7, 1901)		Purported Deed by Erisse & Charles Carbello to Tresimond Granger: signed by mark in Lake Charles, LA before Notary Thomas Kleinpeter, purporting to sell all 300 acres of Block No. 3 to her brother Tresimond Granger for $450 cash; held off the record for 13 years until Oct. 7, 1901.Verbatim in Equity No. 234 Bill of Complaint § IV (OPR #291245, p. 7); cited in DR Vol. 40, p. 204Verified Record Explains why Tresimond Granger and his heirs occupied, subdivided, and leased Block/Subdivision No. 3 between 1888 and 1924. Later adjudged invalid as to Erisse’s capacity in Cause No. 23895 (1924), subject to the court-approved compromise.
June 13, 1913Warranty Deed: Tresimon Granger conveys a 50-acre square (531.4 × 531.4 varas) in the north line of Subdivision No. 3 (and part of Subdivision No. 2) to his son John Granger for $1 and love and affection.Deed Record Vol. 14, p. 241Verified Record Creates the “John Granger 50-acre tract” in Subdivision No. 3, later referenced in the 1924/1926 judgments, the 1929/1930 Sarah Jane Granger confirmations (DR 47:551, 50:304), and the 1931 Corbello-heirs mineral deeds (DR 52:123, 130, 171).
June 18 & Aug. 30, 1913Power of Attorney & 5-Acre Deed: Erisse Carbello (without husband’s joinder) gives a 1/2-interest POA to W. O. Huggins & J. T. Moss, who convey 5 acres off the east end of Share No. 3 on Cow Bayou to Joe Lucas for $250 (who leases to Abe Sokolski on Apr. 18, 1921, Contract Vol. 2 p. 305).POA Vol. C, p. 57 (verbatim in OPR #291245, p. 12); DR Vol. 15, pp. 551–552Verified Record Cites “Block No. 3, as set aside to me in the partition of said Treville Granger estate, as shown in Book E, of the Probate Records of Orange County, Texas.” Cancelled as against Lucas and Sokolski in Equity No. 234 (1921).
Mar. 20, 1915Death of Erisse’s brother Tresimond Granger (husband of Elva Chesson); his children (Mose, John, Frank, Joe, Mary Scales, Millie Caron, Lona Pattillo, Aladin, etc.) claim Subdivision No. 3 under the 1888 deed.OPR Vol. 745, p. 728; DR Vol. 40, p. 204Verified Record Explains why Mose Granger, Sarah J. Granger (widow of John), Frank Granger, Mary Scales, Millie Caron, and Lona Pattillo are the principal Granger defendants in the 1921–1926 suits.
Apr. 1, 1916
(rec. Apr. 24, 1916)		Power of Attorney Coupled with a 1/2 Interest: Charles & Erisse Corbello (Lake Charles, LA) appoint John L. Wasey attorney-in-fact to sue for, lease, or compromise their land claims in the William Dyson League, conveying to Wasey an undivided one-half (1/2) interest in all land and money recovered.Deed Record Vol. 22, pp. 507–509Verified Record Crucial title reduction: vests 50% of all recovered land, minerals, and royalties in John L. Wasey (confirmed by the 1924 judgment at DR Vol. 40, p. 208).
Oct. 16, 1920
(rec. Oct. 19, 1920)		Purported Quitclaim Deed: Nephew Mose Granger procures a quitclaim deed from Erisse Carbello (recited as a “feme sole,” though Charles was living) for $25, quitclaiming “division number Three (3), in the division of the estate of Treville Granger.”Deed Record Vol. 32, p. 623 (verbatim in OPR #291245, p. 10)Verified Record Adjudged fraudulent, null, and void in Cause No. 23895 on October 1, 1924 (DR Vol. 40, p. 205).
Nov. 28, 1921 & Dec. 30, 1921
(rec. Jan. 7, 1922)		Federal Equity No. 234 Order Pro Confesso & Final Decree: In Erisse Carbello et al. v. Abe Sokolski et al., U.S. District Court, E.D. Tex. (Beaumont), Judge W. L. Estes enters decree pro confesso (Nov. 28, 1921) and Final Decree (Dec. 30, 1921) adjudging Erisse Carbello fee-simple owner of the 300-acre Block No. 3 as against nine defaulting defendants (Abe Sokolski, S. A. Emerson, Joe Lucas, Edgar Brown, Lutcher Brown, R. A. Moore, Mrs. E. W. Brown, C. L. Adams, and H. T. Staiti) and perpetually enjoining them. (Companion Equity No. 241 decree entered Nov. 29, 1921 settling 5 acres with Amerada Petroleum Corp., DR Vol. 40, p. 317.)Deed Record Vol. 35, pp. 75–77; Vol. 41, pp. 210–212; full NARA file & plat in OPR #291245Verified Record Quiets title to Block 3 against the nine named defaulting defendants; remaining non-defaulting defendants (Mose Granger, Gulf Production Co., Lee Hager, etc.) are litigated and settled in state court (No. 23895) and dismissed without prejudice from Equity 234 on Feb. 14, 1925.
Dec. 19, 1921
(rec. Jan. 14, 1922)		Confirmation Deed of Division No. 5: Cecile Granger and the five branches of Treville Granger Jr.’s heirs ratify their mutual partition of the northern 300-acre Division No. 5 into five 60-acre strips.Deed Record Vol. 35, pp. 137–139Verified Record Confirms the boundaries of Division No. 5 on the north line of the Dyson League, north of Division No. 4.
July 4, 1923Death of Erisse’s husband, Manuel Carlos (“Charles”) Corbello, in Calcasieu Parish, LA (noted by non-suit in Cause No. 5179, DR Vol. 43, p. 498).DR Vol. 43, p. 498; FamilySearch profile 27XP-6YDVerified Record Confirms Erisse was a widow from July 4, 1923 until her death on February 3, 1930.
Oct. 1, 1924
(rec. Oct. 15, 1924)		Final Judgment in 60th Dist. Ct. No. 23895 (Erisse Carbello et al. v. Mose Granger et al.): (1) finds Erisse of unsound mind since Sept. 1, 1888 and voids the 1920 Mose Granger quitclaim; (2) compromises limitation claims on producing leases in Lot/Subdivision No. 3 by ratifying Gulf Production Co.’s leases on the 29-acre Granger Heirs tract and 50-acre Mose Granger tract and awarding plaintiffs 1/5 of the 1/6 royalty (a 1/30 royalty), 1/5 of bonuses, and a 1/5 mineral/royalty interest across the lands held under the 1888 Tresimond Granger deed; and (3) awards the remainder of Block 3 1/2 to Erisse Carbello as her separate property and 1/2 to John L. Wasey.Deed Record Vol. 40, pp. 204–208 (60th Dist. Ct. Civil Minutes Vol. 12, p. 292)Verified Record The controlling 1924 title & royalty judgment on Block 3: defines the exact fractional mineral/royalty recovery (1/5 of minerals and 1/5 of 1/6 royalty on the Tresimond Granger deed lands; 100% of remaining Block 3 lands) and splits that entire recovery 50% to Erisse Carbello and 50% to John L. Wasey (leaving Erisse with 1/10 of minerals/royalty on the compromised tracts and 1/2 of the uncompromised remainder).
Oct. 1 & Dec. 16, 1924Wasey Attorney Assignments: John L. Wasey assigns 3/4 of his 1/2 recovery to Texas attorneys W. D. Gordon (35%), Oswald S. Parker (20%), and L. H. Marshburn (20%), and fractions of his remaining 1/4 to Louisiana attorneys U. A. Bell and Chas. R. Cline.Deed Record Vol. 40, pp. 187, 354, 355Verified Record Explains the presence of W. D. Gordon, Parker, Marshburn, Bell, and Cline in the Block 3 title chain.
Feb. 14, 1925
(rec. May 23, 1925)		Final Order in Federal Equity No. 234: Judge W. L. Estes orders that the Dec. 30, 1921 Final Decree against the 9 defaulted defendants remain in full force and effect, and dismisses the remaining defendants without prejudice following the Oct. 1, 1924 state-court settlement judgment.Deed Record Vol. 41, pp. 210–212; OPR #291245, pp. 23–25Verified Record Closes Equity No. 234 while preserving the 1921 default judgment and the 1924 state-court compromise.
Mar. 1925Interdiction & Guardianship of Erisse Corbello: Erisse is formally interdicted in the 14th JDC, Calcasieu Parish, LA (with ancillary guardianship in Orange County Probate Case No. 680, filed Mar. 30, 1925); Calcasieu National Bank of Southwest Louisiana appointed curator.Corbello v. Corbello, 171 La. 735 (1930); Orange Co. Probate No. 680Verified Record During 1925–1930, the bank curator collects $24,388 in royalties and bonuses from Erisse’s Orange County oil interests (plus $3,500 initial capital from the Texas guardian).
Sept. 4, 1926

3. The ancestral land description

The Tresimond, Doris, Lovett and Duhon instruments repeatedly describe approximately 300 acres, Block or Subdivision No. 3, from Treville Granger’s estate, within a 900-acre tract in the William Dyson League, Orange County, Texas. The Duhon deed also refers to a subdivision of 1,500 acres.

  • The 300 acres are described as set apart to Erisse Granger Corbello in her father’s estate partition.
  • William Morgan is named as county surveyor for that partition.
  • The historical grant to William Dyson is described as dated February 13, 1835, by Coahuila and Texas.
  • Later records identify the Dyson survey as Abstract No. 10 / A-10.

These are useful matching keys. A lease or deed elsewhere in the same league does not automatically concern this 300-acre tract.

↑ Back to top

4. Trustee deed & challenged historical documents

Dyson Descendants Corporation → Gary W. Duhon, Trustee for the heirs of Erisse Granger Corbello. The deed describes the 300-acre Block 3 tract and purports to bargain, release and warrant the corporation’s rights to the trustee for the heirs.

  • Executed June 18, 1986; filed June 27, 1986.
  • Orange County Volume 624, pages 211–215; instrument 39960.
  • The other window’s handoff called this a “1985 deed.” The execution date visible in the reviewed scan is 1986; the discrepancy is retained here explicitly.

Exhibit A: Spanish Archives

An old Spanish-language land document accompanies a General Land Office certification dated June 17, 1986, identifying Volume 22, pages 303–305, Spanish Archives. A clearer official copy is needed for reliable transcription and translation. The microfilm certificate also appears to contain a year inconsistent with the execution and filing dates.

Exhibit B: examiner’s opinion

PDF page 5 / recorded page 214 contains an August 7, 1985 letter from J. Frank Stubbs, questioned-document examiner, to Mrs. Betty Cayley. He expresses the opinion that historical signatures or marks attributed to William Dyson, John A. Veatch and others were written by different named persons. PDF page 6 contains comparison measurements.

This establishes that an examiner raised and documented those concerns. It is not itself a court judgment establishing forgery.

Follow-up: trustee appointment and authority, trust agreement, beneficiaries, later conveyances, accounting, distributions, termination, successor trustee and related litigation.

Local source: Joseph Lovett/Duhon - William Dyson Descendants.PDF · 7 PDF pages.

5. Federal lawsuit & the “Curious Document”

Equity No. 234

The later family affidavit names Marguerite Erisse Granger Corbello et al. v. Abe Sokolski et al., in the U.S. District Court, Eastern District of Texas, Beaumont, and cites Volume 41, pages 210–212. Update: the entire 25-page NARA-certified Equity No. 234 packet — including the 1921 Bill of Complaint, the Exhibit “A” Partition Plat of the Estate of Treville Granger (Vol. E, pp. 147–150), the December 30, 1921 Final Decree (also recorded January 7, 1922 at Deed Record Vol. 35, pp. 75–77), and the February 14, 1925 Final Decree (Vol. 41, pp. 210–212) — has now been retrieved and read in full from Orange County Official Public Records Instrument #291245 (filed December 13, 2005, certified by NARA Fort Worth on December 2, 2005). Full details and plat breakdown in Section 13B.

The court-copy caption reads Erisse Carbello et al. v. Abe Sokolski et al. The named defaulted defendants include Abe Sokolski, S. A. Emerson, Joe Lucas, Edgar Brown, Lutcher Brown, R. A. Moore, Mrs. E. W. Brown individually and as representative of the E. W. Brown estate, C. L. Adams and H. T. Staiti. Some spellings differ within the typewritten record; the earlier “E. A. Emerson / Joe Lugas” transcription from the 2001 affidavit should not be used as the only search spelling.

Local sources: Documents with Forever/Corbello_1.PDF and Corbello_2.PDF, PDF pages 2–4; recorded September 4, 2001, Vol. 1248 pp. 888–891, instrument 227435.

The 1987 recorded claim

The handwritten instrument labeled “Special Warranty Deed,” No. 55660, was filed November 19, 1987, at Volume 672, pages 289–291. It asserts Corbello-heir possession beginning in June 1983 of 300 acres, Block 3, and cites Book 41 page 211 and “Federal Court Ruling Equity #234.” It associates the older record with 1925; that is a later claimant’s reference, not independently verified case dating.

Robert L. Corbello records a claim to an undivided inherited interest. The clerk’s memo notes unsatisfactory legibility. This is a recorded claim, not the federal judgment, and it contains no payment ledger.

Local sources: Joseph Lovett/Curious_Document.PDF and PDF27751.PDF, visually reproducing the same instrument. The other window also found a matching Curious_Document.PDF on the Samsung USB archive.

Corbello Robin (d. 12/22/1958) were the two children of Martin Corbello (d. September 7, 1939, Lafayette, LA) and Annie Mellen Thompson (married April 20, 1909, Orange County Marriage Book 5 p. 218).

Corbello affidavit · 2001

  • Vol. 1248 pp. 888–891; instrument 227435, recorded September 4, 2001.
  • Names Robert Larry Corbello and Jerry Dean Corbello as the only surviving heirs of Marguerite Arise Erisse Granger Corbello; separately names them as Robert Oval Corbello’s only surviving sons.
  • Affiants are named Robert Larry Corbello and Norma Latham; signatures include Norma L. Corbello.
  • States Marguerite died February 24, 1925; names husband Charles Corbello, deceased July 4, 1923; lists Martin and Trezimond as her only children.
  • Cites earlier heirship Vol. 627 p. 962, instrument 41237 (now retrieved above — which is solely the heirship of Robert Oval Corbello, not of Erisse).
  • Claims possession from June 1983, numerous pumping wells and unpaid mineral-interest amounts. These are claimant statements, not verified production or debt records.

Discrepancies now resolved by primary records: Retrieving Vol. 627 p. 962 (#41237), the other six 1986–2006 branch affidavits, and the November 1931 mineral deeds (DR Vol. 52 pp. 123, 130, 171) proves that Erisse and Charles Corbello had eight children (Tresimond, Mary Modest LeBleu, Artemon, Angeline Champyne, Josephine Duhon, Clarise Lovett, Martin, and Mary Doris Savoie), and that Robert Larry and Jerry Dean Corbello were the two sons of Robert Oval Corbello (grandson of Erisse through her son Martin Corbello), not the sole heirs of Erisse.

The family reports descent through the maternal Hargrove line. A newly supplied tree points specifically through Clarice Corbello, Solomon Lovett and Martha Lovett, rather than through the Hagar branch. The Hagar/Hager interest listings remain separate collateral leads, not presumed ancestors. See the screenshot-to-affidavit match below. Juan Corbello’s position in the lineage remains unestablished in this review.

Documents with Forever/Corbello_1.PDF and Corbello_2.PDF.

9. Updates from the other research window

Imported October 18, 2026 from a separate research session. Its family-research handoffs and available OCR were reviewed for this consolidation. Third-party database reports below retain their status as leads; they are not certified title or payment records.

A Louisiana opinion documents historical oil revenues

Corbello v. Corbello, 171 La. 735, 132 So. 127, Louisiana Supreme Court; opinion December 1, 1930, rehearing denied January 5, 1931. The other window located a case PDF on the Samsung USB. Its OCR was independently read for this page.

  • The opinion describes Mrs. Erisse Corbello obtaining valuable Orange County oil property in 1924, being interdicted in March 1925, and Calcasieu National Bank of Southwest Louisiana serving as curator.
  • Reports her death on February 3, 1930.
  • Reports $24,388 in royalties and bonuses, and $22,645.95 in expenditures during the curatorship.
  • Separately describes $3,500 initial capital received from a Texas guardian; at death, $5,243.04 cash subject to $828.70 charges, or $4,414.34 net, besides a residence and Orange County oil properties.
  • Reports $820 in advances to some appellants. This does not identify all eventual heirs or establish distributions to any specific branch.
  • The bank corrected a $350 commission charge; the judgment also credited the estate $72.50 interest. The judgment approving the account as corrected was affirmed.

Why it matters: this is a court-reported history of actual oil revenue and estate administration, rather than a later lease’s payment clause. It does not itself establish that this Erisse is identical to every similarly named person in the later affidavits, determine current title, or show a government reparations award.

Source: Corbello v. Corbello; local USB case PDF and /tmp/opencode/famdocs/corbello_v_corbello.txt, PDF pages 1–4.

Other reported court leads

  • Chesson v. La Flore, 191 S.W. 745 (Tex. Civ. App. 1916): the full opinion has now been read. It describes approximately the northern 1,500 acres of the William Dyson Survey, five heirs, and a partition; Treville Granger Jr. received 800 acres on the north, later split into 60-acre tracts, and the Jr. heirs’ partition deed to Simeon Michel is dated November 28, 1888. The opinion does not cite the partition’s book and page. The earlier “death in 1887” note is superseded: see section 13B (death November 30, 1881).
  • Gulf Production Co. v. Granger, 57 S.W.2d 1116 (Tex. 1932): reported litigation involving Granger heirs and Dyson League mineral/boundary rights; tract and party matching remain necessary.
  • Dyson Descendant Corp. v. Sonat Exploration Co., 861 S.W.2d 942 (Tex. App. 1993): concerns the James Dyson League, not the William Dyson League. It is not a direct title determination for this 300-acre tract.

Current-production lead: CORBELLO -A- · lease 03-03243

The other window located third-party listings for CORBELLO -A-, Orange field, William Dyson Survey / Abstract 10, with Kilmarnock Oil Company, Inc. listed as operator. The lease identifier is a strong search key, but its overlap with the ancestral Block 3 has not been established from official maps or title documents.

Reported metricThird-party report / limitation
Production periodMineralRecord: January 1993–May 2026; 401 reported months.
Oil / gas / wells98,609 barrels oil; 21 Mcf casinghead gas; six wells reported in the other window’s handoff. Verify units, coverage and well histories with RRC.
Value estimatesMineralRecord: approximately $4.6 million “all-time value,” and $161,000 for the last 12 filed months. These are the service’s estimates, not an unpaid royalty balance, estate valuation or amount owed to the family.
Appraisal/ownership listingNewBizBot: about $237,000 market/appraised value, 289 owners / 290 interest rows. These are a different metric from production value, and the reporting year and official mineral account still need confirmation.

Adams levy proceeds and Karen Harris among interest-row names. Reported row values ranged from $5,060 to $7,780; these are database appraisal entries, not evidence of checks received.

Lead sources: MineralRecord lease 3243 · NewBizBot property listing · MineralAnswers · Texas-Drilling. Direct NewBizBot access encountered a browser challenge.

Hagar / Hager / Hargrove: Lee Hager oil-royalty connection now resolved

MineralHolders lists William Scott Hager with a reported 0.000037 royalty interest in CORBELLO -A- W#16–18, and entries for four other Kilmarnock leases: FEDERAL ROYALTY W#1,3; GORDON, W.D. W#2,4–5; MCLEAN, MARRS “A” W#2,4,6; and WINFREE, WILLIAM W#2,7–8,10–11. “Hager Oil & Gas” was also reported as a mutual-owner lead.

Update (Oct. 19 — resolved): The newly retrieved 1921 Bill of Complaint in Equity No. 234 (OPR #291245, p. 4) and the October 1, 1924 Judgment in Cause No. 23895 (DR Vol. 40, p. 204) name “Lee Hager … a citizen of the State of Texas residing in Harris County, Texas” and Federal Royalty Company as oil-royalty buyers and defendants alongside W. D. Gordon. Lee Hager (1874–1944) was the Houston oil geologist and president of Federal Royalty Company / Hager Oil & Gas who acquired royalty fractions across the Orange Oil Field (including the Corbello, Gordon, McLean, and Winfree leases) in the 1920s. Modern owner William Scott Hager and Hager Oil & Gas trace to Houston oilman Lee Hager, not to John Thomas Hagar (husband of Lillie May Corbello Hagar in the 1986 Tresimond Corbello affidavit) or to the maternal Hargrove line.

Appraisal and portal work

The other window reports 26 CORBELLO real-property account results through orangecad.org, including R12776 (Georgia), R12780 (Robert C.), R12849 (Bennie W.), R12852 (Richard), R12870 (Georgia), R12874 (Jeffery), R12886 (Robert C.) and R12890 (Lillian Corbello Adams). These surface/real-property accounts do not establish the mineral account or ancestral tract.

Portal update: the other window successfully retrieved Book 41 pp. 210–212; the court copies are described in section 13. The OCAD eSearch portal timed out. The full 1921 federal decree and official mineral-account acreage remain outstanding.

The other window reports TexasFile index coverage beginning April 15, 1852, with older deed books and Official Public Records through the early 2000s available by collection. Verify current coverage and fees directly before ordering images.

Excluded false leads

The USB file Granger - New York - Orange County Results.pdf was identified as a 722-page FAA aircraft collateral index, not family genealogy. Several cobello_granger*.png and Calcasieu_Corbello_Hit_Possible*.png images were reported as name-collision or portal screenshots, not authoritative findings. An unrelated Hargrove parcel in Florida was excluded.

10. Priority record searches & legal verification checklist

  1. Original Equity No. 234 & companion suits: the December 30, 1921 final decree in Equity No. 234 has now been located in Orange County Deed Record Vol. 35 pp. 75–77, alongside the November 29, 1921 decree in Equity No. 241 (Erisse Carbello et al. v. Amerada Petroleum Corp., DR Vol. 40 p. 317), the October 1, 1924 judgment in 60th Dist. Ct. No. 23895 (Erisse Carbello et al. v. Mose Granger et al., DR Vol. 40 pp. 204–208), and the September 4, 1926 judgment in Orange County Dist. Ct. No. 5179 (Samuel Duhon et al. v. Erisse Granger Carbello et al., DR Vol. 43 pp. 497–500). Still to obtain from NARA Fort Worth: the Equity No. 234 Bill of Complaint and case file (requested October 19, 2026).
  2. Duhon trusteeship: follow Vol. 624 pp. 211–215, all deeds to/from Gary W. Duhon as trustee, beneficiary lists, trust agreement, accounting and distributions.
  3. Original estate partition — LOCATED IN PROBATE INDEX: contrary to the 1937 family statement that there was no administration, three recorded deeds/judgments (DR Vol. 15 p. 551, Vol. 40 p. 206, Vol. 43 p. 499) and Sheet 70 of the Orange County Index to Probate Minutes establish that Treville Granger Sr.’s estate was administered in Orange County Probate Case No. 15 (filed March 19, 1883; Tresimo Granger, administrator) and partitioned on February 10, 1888, recorded in Probate Minutes Book E, pages 147–150 (Order for Partition p. 147; Report of Partition p. 147; Partition Approved p. 150). Next step: obtain a copy of Probate Minutes Book E, pages 131 and 143–150, and the packet for Probate Case No. 15 from the Orange County Clerk or Sam Houston Center.
  4. Trace post-1924 and post-1930 mineral conveyances: the 1924 judgment (DR Vol. 40 pp. 204–208) compromised the title with Tresimond Granger’s heirs on key producing tracts (awarding Erisse and Wasey 1/5 of the 1/6 royalty and 1/5 of bonus on the 50-acre and 29-acre Gulf Production leases, while recovering the rest of Block 3 half to Erisse and half to Wasey). In November 1931 (DR Vol. 52 pp. 123, 130, 171), seven of Erisse’s children conveyed mineral interests in the 50-acre tract to J. H. Harlan, reserving a 1/96 royalty. Trace all post-1924 conveyances by Erisse’s curator and children to determine what royalty or mineral fraction remained in each branch.
  5. Reconcile identity and heirship: compare the affidavits, Vol. 627 p. 962 / instrument 41237, Louisiana curatorship/succession, and vital records. Prioritize the Corbello–Lovett–Thomas–Hargrove chain supplied in the new tree and clarify Juan’s place in it.
  6. Official lease verification: use RRC 03-03243 and API identifiers for lease maps, well/operator history and production; identify the official OCAD mineral account. Match the footprint to the 50-acre and 29-acre tracts in Lot/Subdivision No. 3 described in the 1924 and 1926 judgments.
  7. Complete archive review: the local family-folder OCR pass (67 PDFs) and 24 county-portal pulls are now indexed locally.

Historical royalty receipts, present production estimates, appraised mineral interests and compensation awards are different kinds of evidence. This notebook does not calculate a family entitlement or promise recovery.

County access checked in this window

Orange County’s official online-records page lists imaged books 1852–1985 and Official Public Records 1985–present. Its JavaScript portal initially returned only a loading page here.

The county’s search-request policy states $5 per name for staff searches by mailed request and payment, and no searches by phone or email. Clerk contact: 409-882-7055; 801 W. Division, Orange, TX 77630. Verify policy before mailing a request.

12. Maternal-lineage evidence: Clarise Corbello Lovett’s branches

Supplied FamilySearch profile screenshots and the newly retrieved June 6, 2006 Orange County OPR heirship batch (#298552–#298565) document two parallel maternal lines descending from Clarice (Clarise) Corbello + Solomon Lovett:

  • Wing 1 — The Martha Olivia Lovett Thomas → Wanda Lea Idella Thomas Self → Francine J. Sherfield (Hargrove) Line: OPR #298552 establishes Clarise Corbello Lovett’s descent to daughter Martha Olivia Lovett Thomas (Jan. 4, 1911 – Jan. 25, 1995); and OPR #298555 (Affidavit of Heirship of Martha Lovett Thomas) explicitly names Wanda Lea Idella Thomas Self, birth July 12, 1938 (d. Aug. 22, 2007; married first to William Hargrove Jr. [1934–1995], father of Francine J. Sherfield [née Francis Jeanette Hargrove], and later to Cecil Enoch Self [1942–2005]) on the Orange County records as one of the five surviving heirs of Martha Lovett Thomas in the 29-acre and 271-acre tracts of Block No. 3.
  • Wing 2 — The Idellar Lovett Gilley → Bertha Mae Gilley Romero → Debbie Romero Vezina Line: OPR #298552 establishes Clarise Corbello Lovett’s descent to eldest daughter Idellar Lovett Gilley (Oct. 3, 1901 – Nov. 25, 1976); and OPR #298557 (Affidavit of Heirship of Idellar Lovett Gilley) explicitly names Bertha Mae Gilley Romero, birth June 15, 1932 (mother of Debbie Romero Vezina) on the Orange County records as one of the six surviving heirs of Idellar Lovett Gilley in the 29-acre and 271-acre tracts of Block No. 3.

These are profile assertions visible in supplied screenshots. Attached-source counts are not a substitute for reading those sources. The next priority is the source tabs for Clarice, Martha and Wanda: census households, birth/death records, marriages and probate identifying the parent-child links.

Direct match to a recorded document: the 1986 affidavit at Vol. 634 pp. 184–185 names Clarise Corbello Lovett, her husband Solomon Lovett and their child Martha Lovett. It states Clarise held an undivided 1/8 surface and mineral interest in the 300-acre Block 3 tract.

This is a specific intersection between the family’s tree and an existing land-related heirship filing. It makes the Lovett branch the priority for documenting this family’s inheritance chain. The screenshot is a supplied genealogy lead; the records attached to the tree and each parent-child link still need to be examined before treating the entire chain as documented heirship.

Identity clue: Manuel Carlos’s profile death date, July 4, 1923, matches the date assigned to “Charles Corbello” in the 2001 affidavit. The court also names Charles Carbello as Erisse’s husband. This supports researching Carlos/Charles name equivalence, but does not establish it without the underlying records. Juan’s connection should be traced separately.

Private sources: Pictures/Screenshots/, overview at 22-23-27 and expanded profiles at 22-27-30, 22-27-40, 22-27-45, 22-27-50, 22-27-54, 22-28-00 and 22-28-04 on October 18, 2026, read directly. Images, account details and additional living-family information are not uploaded.

Parentage of the 2001 affidavit’s named heirs (lead, not verified)

A third-party compiled report, Descendants of Juan Curbelo (CIDA-SA, 2021), gives this line for Manuel Carlos “Charles” Corbello (d. 1923 per family records) and Marguerite Erise Granger (d. February 3, 1930, Sallier Cemetery, Lake Charles): their son Martin Corbello (1890–1939), whose son Robert Oval Corbello (1911–1986, Vidor, Orange County) is recorded as the father of the two men named in the 2001 affidavit. The affidavit itself also names them as Robert Oval Corbello’s sons, which is consistent with the report.

Status: this is a lead. The report’s earlier generations, the duplicate Manuel Carlos entry (listed under two different parents) and the omission of several of Marguerite’s children are unresolved. The chain does not establish heirship, title or entitlement to any royalty. Verify each generation against baptism, marriage, death and probate records before relying on it.

Source: https://cida-sa.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/Curbelo-Family-Descendants-Report.pdf. Find a Grave memorial 14712720 (marker reads “ERISE GRANGER CORBELLO, AUG. 3, 1849, FEB. 3, 1930”).

12A. Maternal and collateral branches: Tresimond → Hagar

A further supplied screenshot explicitly displays Tresimond Corbello + Sarah Ann Cole → Lillie Mae Corbello + John Thomas Hagar, alongside Josephine Blanche Corbello + Jay Seay Benniefiel. Clarice Corbello appears on the same sibling row as Tresimond.

This closely matches the 1986 affidavit’s family structure: Tresimond and Sarah Cole, daughters Lillie May Corbello Hagar Bertrand and Josephine Blanche Corbello Benniefiel, with Hagar and Benniefiel descendants. Mae/May spelling, spouse names and any subsequent Bertrand marriage should be checked using the attached records.

Significance: the supplied tree and recorded affidavit now align on a specific Hagar connection. The tree portrays the Hagar line as a collateral branch related to the family’s displayed Clarice → Martha Lovett → Wanda Thomas → Hargrove maternal line. It does not by itself connect the modern William Scott Hager mineral listing to these Hagar descendants, or establish any branch’s current royalty fraction.

Private source: Pictures/Screenshots/Screenshot From 2026-10-18 22-34-28.png, read directly; compared with Tresimond Corbello/affadavit_of_heirship.pdf, Vol. 622 pp. 767–768.

13. Late-evening court & archival findings

Book 41 pp. 210–212 now retrieved

The other research window downloaded these pages from Orange County’s Imaged Books collection; this window read the page images directly. The official record is Erisse Carbello et al. v. Abe Sokolski et al., Equity No. 234, U.S. District Court, Eastern District of Texas at Beaumont. This is not the original Granger estate partition.

  1. November 28, 1921: certified order pro confesso against named defendants; recorded starting on page 210.
  2. December 30, 1921: the later decree recites that a final decree was entered, recorded in federal minutes December 31, awarding complainants the relief prayed for against named defendants. Update (Oct. 19): that December 30, 1921 final decree has now been retrieved in full from Orange County Deed Record Volume 35, pages 75–77 (see Section 13B).
  3. February 14, 1925: Judge W. L. Estes orders the 1921 final decree to “stand and remain in full force and effect,” unaffected by dismissal of the remaining defendants. Those remaining defendants are dismissed without prejudice to complainants’ rights against them.
  4. May 9, 1925: federal clerk J. R. Blades, by deputy Susie Kerr, certifies the copies. Filed in Orange County May 13 and recorded May 23, 1925.

The 1925 decree recites that Erisse Carbello, by next friend John L. Wasey, joined pro forma by husband Charles Carbello, and John L. Wasey individually, alleged fee-simple ownership of the described 300-acre Block 3. It explicitly connects the tract to Erisse’s portion of father Treville Granger’s partition and county surveyor William Morgan.

Meaning for the claim: this is contemporaneous court-record evidence of relief granted against specific defendants. It is stronger than a later assertion of ownership. It does not establish a present-day share, bind unnamed successors automatically, or show that Erisse alone owned every interest: Wasey was also an individual complainant. Obtain his interest documents, the original complaint, the full 1921 decree and subsequent conveyances.

Retrieved scans: /tmp/opencode/famdocs/Book41_Page210.pdf, Book41_Page211.pdf, Book41_Page212.pdf. Downloads contain overlapping adjacent pages. Official access: Orange County Clerk online records, Imaged Books Before 1985, DEED volume 0041 pages 0210–0212. No expiring portal-session identifiers are published.

Specific National Archives series identified

The National Archives’ Eastern District of Texas finding aid lists these Beaumont Division collections:

CollectionNARA identifierLocal identifier
Equity Case Files, 1898–193857324721-ETX-007H
Equity Dockets, 1897–193858118121-ETX-007F
Equity Minutes, August 1897–October 1943450469921-ETX-007C
Index to Plaintiffs and Defendants in Equity Cases, 1922–1930450470821-ETX-007G

These are verified collection descriptions, not confirmation that the individual case file has been located or digitized. Because this suit existed by 1921, the 1922–1930 party index may not include its initial filing; the docket and December 1921 minutes are especially important. Archival research contact: ftworth.archives@nara.gov, 817-551-2051. Request Equity 234 using Carbello/Corbello and Sokolski spelling variants, Wasey, November–December 1921 and February 1925. A written request was sent to Fort Worth on October 19, 2026; see section 13A.

Granger settlement payments described in a published opinion

This window read the full Gulf Production Co. v. Granger opinion and rehearing. It identifies Mose Granger et al. v. Gulf Production Company et al., Orange County District Court No. 4397, and describes:

  • A January 1922 settlement with Humble for cash plus a stated 2% oil-payment interest on a disputed 10⅔-acre strip.
  • A January 19, 1922 settlement with Gulf and its lessors for $10,000 cash plus 1/96 of oil on specified lease lands excluding that strip; a conditional 1/40 oil interest concerned the strip.
  • The court rejected the argument that the limited Gulf/Humble compromise alone triggered the contract’s suit-settlement exception. On rehearing, it expressly did not decide liability under another contract provision; this should not be summarized as a blanket award of ongoing royalties.
  • Named beneficiaries/participants include Mose Granger, Mary Scales, Millie Caron, Sarah Jane Granger, Ada Granger, and specified minors, with assignments to John B. Warren and J. A. Conn discussed.

The disputed strip ultimately was held to be in the James Dyson League; the litigation involved the boundary with the William Dyson League. These contractual payment interests are a separate lead, not established payments on Erisse’s Block 3 or proof that this family is a beneficiary. Obtain No. 4397 and the original contracts before making that connection.

Boundary history read directly

Chesson v. La Flore describes Treville Granger Sr.’s five heirs and a northern 300-acre allotment to Treville Granger Jr., subsequently split into 60-acre tracts. That northern allotment must not be conflated with Erisse’s Block 3 solely because both are 300 acres. The decision reversed and rendered a five-acre title dispute in favor of the appellant; it did not award this family the whole league.

Production listings independently checked

The MineralRecord lease page and William Scott Hager listing were fetched directly here. They reproduce the figures described above. MineralRecord’s 98,609-barrel figure is labeled oil disposition, not simply well production; its dollar total is volume multiplied by published prices, not actual royalty checks. It places five located wellbores around 30.06946, −93.84968, with a sixth unlocated. These coordinates are a mapping lead, not a lease boundary.

Official route for royalty and historical production research

The RRC’s Royalties FAQ confirms that its production and permit records can help identify wells, while lease and royalty-payment rights are private matters outside its adjudicatory authority. It directs pre-January-1993 production and offline historical permit requests to Central Records, 512-463-6800, and unclaimed royalty searches to the Texas Comptroller’s ClaimItTexas. Official query menu provides Wellbore, Production, P-4 purchaser and imaged-record tools. Targeted official lease retrieval remains ongoing; third-party listings are not a substitute for title or payor records.

13A. Records requests sent — October 19, 2026

Both requests were sent from the family research address on October 19, 2026. The wording actually submitted is reproduced below so later replies can be matched to it. Signature blocks are omitted in keeping with this page’s practice of not publishing personal identifiers.

National Archives at Fort Worth

  • To: ftworth.archives@nara.gov · 817-551-2051
  • Sent: October 19, 2026
  • Subject: Reference request: RG 21, Beaumont Division, Equity Case No. 234 (Erisse Corbello/Carbello v. Abe Sokolski et al.)
  • Acknowledgment: automated receipt the same day. NARA at Fort Worth states that a staff member will respond within ten (10) business days.
  • Requested: the complete Equity Case File No. 234 (series 573247 / 48E007H); the December 31, 1921 Equity Minutes entry (series 4504699 / 021-ETX-007C); the Equity Docket entry (series 581181 / 48E007F); a cost estimate; on-site/off-site status; and any bill, decree or exhibit that recites the recording of the Treville Granger Sr. estate partition.
Text of the NARA request

Dear National Archives at Fort Worth,

I am researching a chain of title in Orange County, Texas, and request assistance locating and copying records from Record Group 21, U.S. District Court for the Eastern District of Texas, Beaumont Division.

The case is:

Erisse Carbello (also spelled Corbello), et al. v. Abe Sokolski, et al.
U.S. District Court for the Eastern District of Texas, at Beaumont
Equity Case No. 234
Final decree dated December 30, 1921, filed and recorded in the court’s minutes on December 31, 1921.

The complainants were Erisse Carbello, by her next friend John L. Wasey, joined pro forma by her husband Charles Carbello, and John L. Wasey individually. The bill alleged that Erisse owned, in her separate right, 300 acres known as Block No. 3 of the subdivision of the Treville Granger Nine Hundred Acre tract in the William Dyson Survey, Orange County, Texas. The named defendants included Abe Sokolski, S. A. Emerson, Joe Lucas, Edgar Brown, Lutcher Brown, R. A. Moore, Mrs. E. W. Brown, C. L. Adams, and H. T. Staiti.

A certified copy of a February 14, 1925 order in the same case was recorded in Orange County Deed Records, Volume 41, pages 210-212. That order recites the December 30, 1921 final decree and states that it awarded the complainants all relief prayed for. I am trying to obtain the actual 1921 decree and the related docket/minute entries. In particular, I hope the bill of complaint or decree will recite the date and recording reference of the partition of the estate of Treville Granger, Sr., deceased.

Please search for and provide copies of:

  1. The complete Equity Case File No. 234, including the final decree dated December 30, 1921. Series: Equity Case Files, 1898-1938. NARA ID: 573247. Local identifier: 48E007H (finding aid: 21-ETX-007H).
  2. The December 31, 1921 entry in the Equity Minutes for Case No. 234. Series: Equity Minutes, 8/1897-10/1943. NARA ID: 4504699. Local identifier: 021-ETX-007C (finding aid: 21-ETX-007C).
  3. The Equity Docket entry for Case No. 234. Series: Equity Dockets, 1897-1938. NARA ID: 581181. Local identifier: 48E007F (finding aid: 21-ETX-007F).

The case file series is arranged numerically by case number, 1 to 564, so No. 234 should fall within it. The Equity Minutes are arranged chronologically by court session, so the December 31, 1921 entry should be identifiable from the date and case number.

Please let me know whether these records are on-site or off-site, the estimated cost for certified copies, and any order or payment steps required. If the case file is not found under No. 234, please check the docket and index for the case style above and let me know the correct file number.

Thank you for your assistance.

Sam Houston Regional Library and Research Center

  • To: SamHoustonCenter@tsl.texas.gov · 936-336-8821 · 650 FM 1011, Liberty, TX
  • Sent: October 19, 2026
  • Subject: Research request: Orange County records, Treville Granger Sr. estate partition (ca. 1883-1890)
  • Requested: Deed Record Index 1846–1889, especially Deed Record Volumes H (later pages), I and J; District Clerk Civil Docket 1879–1949 and Civil Case Papers 1857–1974 for a partition or estate case; and Civil Minutes 1852–1985 for a partition decree or order.
Text of the Sam Houston Center request

Dear Sam Houston Regional Library and Research Center,

I am researching the chain of title to a 300-acre tract in the William Dyson Survey, Orange County, Texas, and would like to request a records search.

The tract is described as Block No. 3 of the subdivision of the Treville Granger Nine Hundred Acre tract, also described as Block No. 3 of the subdivision of Fifteen Hundred acres belonging to the Estate of Treville Granger, deceased, according to a survey made by William Morgan, County Surveyor, in the partition of said land. A 1925 certified copy of a federal decree in Equity Case No. 234, U.S. District Court at Beaumont, recites that this 300 acres was set aside to Erisse Granger Corbello in the partition of the estate of her deceased father, Treville Granger. A 1959 partition case in Orange County District Court, No. 12,264, Stanley Lee Granger v. Ovie Eudale Granger, et al., also refers to “Lot No. 3 of the partition of the Estate of Treville Granger, Sr., deceased.”

Treville Granger Sr. (Jean Treville Granger) died November 30, 1881, in Lake Charles, Calcasieu Parish, Louisiana. He left no will and, I am advised, there was no administration of his estate. His surviving children/heirs were Treville Granger Jr. (died 1879; his children took his share), Michel (Michael) Granger, Tresimond Granger, Erisse Granger Corbello, and Martin Granger. The heirs acquired the 900-acre tract by a confirmation deed from Moise LeBleu dated June 2, 1883, recorded in Orange County Deed Records, Volume H, pages 560-561. The partition therefore occurred after June 1883, and likely between 1883 and 1888.

Please search the Orange County records in your custody for any partition, estate, heirship, or civil case involving Treville Granger Sr. or his heirs, ca. 1883-1890. In particular, please check:

  1. Deed Record Index, 1846-1889 (County Clerk), for any partition deed or instrument involving Treville Granger, his estate, or his heirs, especially Deed Record Volumes H (later pages), I, and J.
  2. District Clerk Civil Docket, 1879-1949, and Civil Case Papers, 1857-1974, for a partition or estate case involving Treville Granger Sr. or his heirs.
  3. Civil Minutes, 1852-1985, for any decree or order of partition.

I am trying to identify the case name and number, the date of the partition, and the volume/page where the partition decree or deed was recorded. Please let me know whether you can locate a matching case, the estimated cost for copies or a research fee, and the applicable payment procedure.

Thank you for your assistance.

↑ Back to top

13B. Primary-record breakthroughs — October 19, 2026

1. Estate Partition of Treville Granger Sr. LOCATED · Probate Case No. 15, Probate Minutes Book E, pp. 147–150 (Feb. 10, 1888)

Three newly retrieved Orange County instruments — Deed Record Vol. 15 p. 551 (Aug. 30, 1913), Vol. 40 p. 206 (Oct. 1, 1924), and Vol. 43 p. 499 (Sept. 4, 1926) — independently cite the original partition of Treville Granger Sr.’s estate as recorded in the Probate Minutes of the Probate Court of Orange County, Texas, in Book “E”, pages 147 et seq. Using that citation, we located the complete docket entry on Sheet 70 (Gr–Gu) of the Orange County Index to Probate Minutes (orange_probate_index.pdf, PDF pp. 99–100):

  • Case No. 15 — Estate of Treville Granger, Deceased · Filed March 19, 1883 · Administrator: Tresimo Granger.
  • March 19, 1883: Order appointing administrator (Probate Minutes Book D, p. 170); May 21, 1883: Bond & oath and approval (D 181); November 3, 1886: Order appointing appraisers (D 362).
  • February 21 – August 1, 1887: Annual account approved (E 8); Inventory on May 17, 1887 (E 32); orders on account on May 18 and August 1, 1887 (E 37, E 82).
  • February 10, 1888: Amended final account (E 143); Citation (E 145); Final account approved (E 146); Order for Partition (Book E, p. 147); Report of Partition (Book E, p. 147); and Partition Approved (Book E, p. 150).
  • Companion entries on the same index sheet (Sheet 70): (a) Estate of Granger Minors: Modesty, Izami, Tresimo & Martene, filed August 3, 1885, Cecile Granger guardian (D 252–254, D 311, E 150, E 366); and (b) Estate of Eddie [Erisse] Granger, N.C.M. (Non Compos Mentis), filed January 6, 1888, Lunacy Proceedings (Probate Minutes Book E, p. 131).

Why it matters: this resolves the central mystery of the partition. Contrary to Frank Granger’s 1937 recollection that there was no administration, Treville Granger Sr.’s estate was administered in Orange County Probate Case No. 15 from 1883 to 1888 and partitioned by probate decree on February 10, 1888 (Book E, pp. 147–150), just one month after the January 6, 1888 lunacy entry (Book E, p. 131).

2. Complete Federal Equity No. 234 NARA Packet & Exhibit “A” Partition Plat LOCATED · OPR #291245 (Dec. 13, 2005) & DR Vol. 35, pp. 75–77

In addition to the December 30, 1921 Final Decree recorded January 7, 1922 at Orange County Deed Record Volume 35, pages 75–77, we located a 25-page NARA-certified reproduction of the Equity No. 234 file (certified December 2, 2005 by Barbara Rust for Kent C. Carter, Regional Administrator, NARA Southwest Region, Fort Worth) recorded in Orange County Official Public Records on December 13, 2005 as Instrument #291245. It contains:

  • The complete 1921 Bill of Complaint (pp. 2–17): filed by solicitors Chas. R. Cline and U. A. Bell of Lake Charles, La. for complainants Erisse Carbello (by next friend John L. Wasey), Charles Carbello, and John L. Wasey individually against Abe Sokolski, Mose Granger, Joe Granger, Sarah J. Granger (individually and as guardian of minor children of John Granger), Aladin, Albert C., and W. R. Granger, Lessie Granger Walker, Mary Scales, Millie Caron, Lona Pattillo, S. A. Emerson, George Blanch, John Chesson, W. M. Gunstream, J. N.igs, J. T. Hilliard, Joe Lucas, C. L. Adams, Edgar Brown, Lutcher Brown, R. A. Moore, Mrs. E. W. Brown, Gulf Production Company, H. T. Staiti, Lee Hager, W. O. Huggins, E. L. Nall, C. T. Heisig, R. S. Barber, and Atlantic Oil Producing Company.
  • Verbatim text of the September 8, 1888 Deed from Charles & Erisse Carbello to Tresimond Granger (Bill of Complaint § IV, p. 7): executed by mark at Lake Charles, Calcasieu Parish, LA before Notary Thomas Kleinpeter (witnesses Cladin Granger and Daniel Goodman), conveying “Block No. 3 of the 900 acres of the Wm. Dyson League in said Orange County according to a survey made by Wm. Morgan, County Surveyor, in partition of said tract containing 300 acres” to her brother Tresimond Granger for $450.00 cash. The Bill notes that this deed “was never filed for record until October 7, 1901,” and alleges that Erisse was of unsound mind and that the Louisiana acknowledgment failed to comply with Texas law for a married woman’s separate-property conveyance.
  • Verbatim text of the June 18, 1913 Power of Attorney to W. O. Huggins & J. T. Moss (Bill § VI, pp. 11–13, recorded POA Vol. C p. 57): recites “my interest in said survey supposed to be Block No. 3, as set aside to me in the partition of said Treville Granger estate, as shown in Book E, of the Probate Records of Orange County, Texas,” and was used by Huggins & Moss to convey 5 acres on Cow Bayou to Joe Lucas on August 30, 1913 (DR Vol. 15, pp. 551–552), who leased to Abe Sokolski on April 18, 1921 (Contract Vol. 2, p. 305).
  • THE EXHIBIT “A” PLAT OF THE PARTITION OF THE ESTATE OF TREVILLE GRANGER (p. 18): titled “— PLAT — SHOWING PARTITION OF Estate of Treville Granger, A part of Wm. Dyson Lgue, Orange County Texas, Vol. E, pp. 147–150 — Exhibit ‘A’.” It maps all five 300-acre divisions from South to North, bounded on the East by Cow Bayou and on the Southwest by Big Marie:
    1. Division No. 1 — Michael Granger — 300 Ac. (southernmost strip along Big Marie and Cow Bayou)
    2. Division No. 2 — Tresimo Granger — 300 Ac. (immediately north of Division No. 1, extending east to Cow Bayou)
    3. Division No. 3 — Erissie Carbello — 300 Ac. (the middle strip, between Division No. 2 [Tresimo Granger] on the south and Division No. 4 [Martine Granger] on the north, extending east to Cow Bayou)
    4. Division No. 4 — Martine Granger — 300 Ac. (north of Division No. 3, extending east to Cow Bayou and wrapping around the east side of Division No. 5)
    5. Division No. 5 — Cecile Granger – Guardian of Minor Heirs of Treville Granger dec’d — 300 Ac. (the northwest rectangular block)
  • The December 30, 1921 Final Decree (pp. 20–21, also recorded Jan. 7, 1922 at DR Vol. 35, pp. 75–77) & February 14, 1925 Final Decree (pp. 23–25, also recorded May 23, 1925 at DR Vol. 41, pp. 210–212): decrees Erisse Carbello owner in fee simple of the 300-acre Block No. 3 as against the nine defaulted defendants (Abe Sokolski, S. A. Emerson, Joe Lucas, Edgar Brown, Lutcher Brown, R. A. Moore, Mrs. E. W. Brown, C. L. Adams, and H. T. Staiti) and dismisses the remaining defendants without prejudice after the October 1, 1924 state-court settlement Judgment in Cause No. 23895.
  • Companion Equity No. 241 Decree (recorded Dec. 4, 1924 at DR Vol. 40, pp. 317–319): entered November 29, 1921 in Erisse Carbello et al. v. Amerada Petroleum Corporation, ratifying Amerada’s lease on the east 5 acres of a 10-acre tract out of the John Granger 50-acre tract in Subdivision No. 3, without prejudice to complainants’ right to recover the 1/6 royalty from adverse claimants.

3. The 1888 Deed to Tresimond Granger & the October 1, 1924 Judgment in No. 23895 · DR Vol. 40, pp. 204–208

Orange County Deed Record Volume 40, pages 204–208 (filed October 15, 1924) preserves a certified copy of the final judgment dated October 1, 1924 in Erisse Carbello et al. v. Mose Granger et al., Cause No. 23895, 60th Judicial District Court of Jefferson County, Texas. It explains how Subdivision/Block No. 3 came to be held and subdivided by Tresimond Granger’s family between 1888 and 1924, and how Erisse recovered her 1924 oil interests:

  • Mental incapacity & voided deeds: the Court expressly finds that “Erisse Carbello is, was and has been ever since the first day of September 1888 and prior to the deed by her to Tresimond Granger dated September 8th, in the year 1888, a person of unsound mind and incapable of binding herself by contract,” and cancels as null and void her October 16, 1920 quitclaim deed to Tresimond’s son Mose Granger (recorded at DR Vol. 32, p. 623, also retrieved and read here).
  • Compromise on producing leases in Lot/Subdivision No. 3: because Tresimond Granger’s heirs and their lessees asserted Texas statutes-of-limitation title under the September 8, 1888 deed and large producing wells on adjacent lands were draining the oil, the Court approved a settlement on the leased portions of Lot No. 3:
    • On the 29-acre “Granger Heirs tract” in the east part of Subdivision No. 3 on Cow Bayou (leased to Gulf Production Company, with royalty claimants including W. M. Gunstream, Lee Hager, and Federal Royalty Company): plaintiffs Erisse Carbello and John L. Wasey recover one-fifth (1/5) of the one-sixth (1/6) royalty (both under the 1/7 Gunstream/Federal Royalty interest and the 6/7 Granger-lessors interest), one-fifth (1/5) of the $1,000-per-acre oil bonus, and a 1/5 mineral reversion if Gulf ceases operations.
    • On the 50-acre Mose Granger tract in Lot No. 3 (immediately east of the 50-acre tract Tresimond Granger deeded to his son John Granger on June 13, 1913 at DR Vol. 14, p. 241; leased to J. N. Pattillo and assigned through F. L. Phair and H. T. Staiti to Gulf Production Company): the Gulf lease is ratified, with one-fifth (1/5) of the royalties and overriding interests retained by Mose Granger and H. T. Staiti adjudged to plaintiffs and 4/5 to Mose Granger and Staiti; across the lands held under the 1888 Tresimond Granger deed, defendants retain surface/valid-deed title except for a one-fifth (1/5) mineral and royalty interest specifically adjudged to plaintiffs.
  • Recovery of the remainder of Block 3 & 50% award to John L. Wasey: except as compromised above, Erisse Carbello recovers title and possession of the 300-acre Block No. 3 (surveyed by County Surveyor William Morgan in the partition of Treville Granger’s estate) as her separate property, “except as to an equal undivided one-half thereof, which it is hereby ordered, adjudged and decreed belong to and are hereby vested in the plaintiff, John L. Wasey, in his individual capacity” as compensation for his services and financial outlay as next friend and under the April 1, 1916 Power of Attorney (DR Vol. 22, pp. 507–509).
  • Wasey’s subsequent assignments (Oct.–Dec. 1924): on October 1, 1924 (DR Vol. 40, p. 187), Wasey conveyed 3/4 of his 1/2 recovery to his Texas counsel W. D. Gordon (35%), Oswald S. Parker (20%), and L. H. Marshburn (20%); on December 16, 1924 (DR Vol. 40, pp. 354–355), Wasey transferred fractions of his remaining 1/4 to Lake Charles attorneys U. A. Bell and Chas. R. Cline. (This directly explains why W. D. Gordon and Federal Royalty Company appear alongside Corbello leases in modern Orange Field records.)

4. September 4, 1926 Judgment in Cause No. 5179 · DR Vol. 43, pp. 497–500

In Samuel Duhon et al. v. Erisse Granger Carbello et al., No. 5179, District Court of Orange County, Texas (recorded September 15, 1926 at DR Vol. 43, pp. 497–500), numerous Duhon, LeBleu, Benoit, Ogea and Billeaud plaintiffs sued Erisse Granger Carbello (represented by her Louisiana curator, Calcasieu National Bank of Southwest Louisiana; the judgment notes that her husband Charles Carbello had died and a non-suit was taken as to him), Wasey, Gordon, and the Granger heirs for the 300-acre Subdivision No. 3. By consent judgment, the plaintiffs recovered only a 1/50 oil interest from H. T. Staiti on the 50-acre Mose Granger tract ($81.75 accrued) and 1/50 from Frank Granger on the 29-acre Granger Heirs tract ($429.32 accrued), and took nothing against Erisse Granger Carbello or her curator.

5. November 1931 Mineral Deeds by Erisse’s Children · DR Vol. 52, pp. 123, 130, 171

Three mineral deeds recorded in November 1931 (after Erisse’s February 3, 1930 death) at Deed Record Volume 52, pages 123, 130, and 171 show seven of Erisse’s children — Mary LeBleu (and husband Colin LeBleu), Angeline Champyne (and husband D. Champyne), Dorie [Doris] Savoie (and husband Tan Savoie), T. C. [Tresimond] Carbello, A. [Artemon] Carbello, Martin Carbello, and Clairese Lovett (and husband Sol Lovett), all of Calcasieu Parish, Louisiana — conveying to J. H. Harlan their undivided mineral interest (warranted at not less than 2.188 undivided acres) in the 50-acre John Granger tract in the north line of Subdivision No. 3 (citing the June 13, 1913 deed at Vol. 14 p. 241 and the Feb. 3, 1925 McMahon lease at Contract Vol. 6 p. 50), reserving to those grantors, their heirs and assigns, a one ninety-sixth (1/96) net royalty on all oil, gas, and minerals produced.

Direct significance for the family’s heirship and mineral records: these 1931 deeds (1) prove by contemporaneous Texas deed records that T. C. (Tresimond) Carbello, Martin Carbello, Artemon Carbello, Mary LeBleu, Angeline Champyne, Doris Savoie, and Clairese Lovett were all recognized surviving children and heirs of Erisse (directly refuting the 2001 affidavit’s claim that Martin and Tresimond were her only children), and (2) document a specific reserved 1/96 royalty in favor of those seven children (including Clairese Lovett and Sol Lovett and T. C. Carbello) on the 50-acre Subdivision No. 3 tract.

6. Complete Documentation of All Eight Children of Erisse Granger Corbello & Charles Corbello

Combining the February 7, 1930 obituary, the November 1931 mineral deeds (DR Vol. 52, pp. 123, 130, 171), and the seven 1986–2006 Orange County OPR heirship affidavits now retrieved, all eight of Erisse and Charles Corbello’s children are documented in official county records:

7. Resolution of the “Two Corbello Families” in the William Dyson League · DR Vol. 34 p. 460, Vol. 35 pp. 77 & 359, Vol. 42 p. 74, OPR Vol. 762 p. 442 & Vol. 892 p. 168

Earlier records in the family folders (such as the 1988 LeBleu donation in Forever_1.PDF, Vol. 689 pp. 118–121, and the 1997 C & E Land Co. deeds in the Bank folder) and modern OCAD / CORBELLO -A- ownership rows feature Jerry Henry Corbello (and wife Georgia Allen Corbello), Bennie E. Corbello Jr. (and sons Robert C., Richard A., and Bennie W. Corbello), Lillian Corbello Adams, and Mary Corbello Stacy (and daughter Madelyn Stacy McCallister). Five newly pulled records establish how that branch fits into the William Dyson League:

  • DR Vol. 42, pp. 74–78 (Nov. 14, 1925 — Certified Copy of Probate Proceedings, Tutorship No. 2262, 15th Judicial District Court, Calcasieu Parish, LA): proves that Augustus (“Gus”) Corbello, Marion L. Corbello, and Benjamin E. (“Bennie”) Corbello Sr. were the three minor children of Esperance Corbello and his deceased wife Madeline (Madilene) Michel (for whom Esperance was appointed natural tutor on August 27, 1913).
  • DR Vol. 34, pp. 460–464 (Nov. 14, 1921 — Judgment in Cause No. 3775, W. M. Gunstream v. Jane Michel et al.) & DR Vol. 35, pp. 77–78 (Jan. 7, 1922 — Federal Equity No. 236, McLean Oil Company v. George A. Foreman et al.): prove that the heirs of Madilene Michel Carbello (Augustus, Marion, and Bennie Carbello) held 8.5-acre and 4.725-acre tracts conveyed by Anna Michel inside the 211-acre William & Delaide Ashworth Reserve in the Northeast corner of the William Dyson League (the 211 acres reserved by the Ashworths when they conveyed the 900 acres to Moise LeBleu on September 10, 1857).
  • OPR Vol. 762, p. 442 (#84047, Sept. 13, 1990 — Will of Jerry Henry Corbello) & OPR Vol. 892, p. 168 (#124742, Jan. 14, 1994 — Heirship of Bennie E. Corbello Jr.): confirm that Jerry Henry Corbello (husband of Georgia Allen Corbello), Bennie E. Corbello Jr. (d. Nov. 22, 1993; father of Robert C., Richard A., and Bennie W. Corbello), Lillian Corbello Adams, and Mary Corbello Stacy (mother of Madelyn Stacy McCallister) are the children of Benjamin E. (“Bennie”) Corbello Sr. (the four minor wards of 1932 Orange County Probate Case No. 919).

Why this distinction is critical: the Esperance Corbello / Madilene Michel / Benjamin E. Corbello family (Branch B) owned land in the 211-acre Ashworth Reserve in the NE corner of the William Dyson League, whereas Charles Corbello & Marguerite Erisse Granger Corbello (Branch A) owned the 300-acre Division / Block No. 3 of the Treville Granger Sr. Estate. Records and royalty accounts belonging to the Esperance/Benjamin E. Corbello branch must not be conflated with Erisse Granger Corbello’s Block 3.

8. Other 1883–2003 records pulled & verified on October 19

  • 1883 LeBleu confirmation deed · Book H pp. 560–561: dated June 2, 1883 (recorded June 25, 1883), Moise L. LeBleu confirms to the five branches of heirs of Treville Granger Sr., deceased (heirs of Treville Jr., dec’d; Michael Granger; Trasimond Granger; Erisse Corbello (Granger); and Martin Granger) the 900 acres in the William Dyson League, east side of Cow Bayou.
  • January 14, 1922 confirmation deed · DR Vol. 35 p. 137: Cecile Granger and the five branches of Treville Granger Jr.’s heirs confirm their mutual partition of the northern 300-acre Division No. 5 of the Treville Granger Sr. estate into five 60-acre strips.
  • 1937 Frank Granger statement · recorded 1990, Vol. 745 p. 728: Paul Cormier affidavit reproducing Frank Granger’s October 9, 1937 family history (six children born to Treville Sr. and Modesti LeBlieu; five survived; death date November 30, 1881).
  • 2003 Duhon adverse-possession notice · Vol. 1364 p. 816 & 2000 pipeline lis pendens · Vol. 1203 p. 951: Bill Duhon’s 2003 notice on 300 acres and Enterprise Lou-Tex NGL Pipeline’s 2000 condemnation lis pendens on the 29.686-acre Paul Cormier tract (Vol. 306 p. 441).
